# Audit of the research articles and browser reading experience

## Latest continuation: Adder-DCIM paper-level reconciliation — 2026-09-09

The emerging-memory-and-devices chapter now connects ICCAD 2025 `1` to an
individual correction record. It separates compressed-model behavior and
bit-flexible representation from faithful digital-CIM execution and complete
inference efficiency, preserves the reported 28-nm, 134/6.49, 255/243 and
below-0.4% source claims with their unresolved conditions, and makes accuracy,
operation-count and power-boundary gaps explicit.

The focused Adder-DCIM test passed, the packet, assignment and manifest
rebuilds completed, and the project validator passed. Corrections are now 568;
manifest artifacts remain 527 and the suite collects 745 tests. This is a
paper-level comprehension pass, not independent reproduction, scientific
acceptance or completion of the broader research program.

The architecture-modeling-and-exploration chapter now gives both selected records individual explanations. ## Latest continuation: DSPE paper-level comprehension pass — 2026-09-08

DSPE now has a full version-1 explanation in the specialized-ai-accelerators chapter. It separates three reductions that the paper combines: MIPS reduces repeated memory access and vector work through incremental similarity ordering, locality-sensitive hash hierarchy and thresholded reuse; MBLM reduces multiplication work through near-zero handling, bit-variation classification, radix-4/radix-8 Booth selection and lookup reuse; DAPPM changes numeric representation through dynamic adaptive Posit fields and 16/9/4-PE paths. The explanation does not treat their combined headline as a single causal result.

The MIPS section makes the evidence boundary explicit: a locality-sensitive hash can group similar inputs and intentionally produce related codes, while a cryptographic integrity result needs a defined threat model, trusted reference, collision properties and mismatch handling. The paper's offline root-hash recomputation interface is therefore presented as a proposed consistency mechanism, not a demonstrated security evaluation. The chapter also distinguishes MIPS's 33.5% DRAM and 36.2% SRAM access reductions, MBLM's 39.1% calculation reduction and DAPPM's 1.47× computation-speed claim from end-to-end latency, energy and task-quality evidence.

The implementation section records four 64-PE attention cores, the parameter/weight buffers and Q/K/V SRAMs, 28 nm synthesis and placement/routing, 8.23 mm² area, 122–345 mW, 710 MHz at 1.10 V, 22.8 TFLOPS at POSIT8 and 109.4 TFLOPS/W at 0.6 V/200 MHz. It preserves the manuscript-versus-description 109.4/91.7 discrepancy and the mismatched Table 1 citation where the H100 reference [1] is a DeepSeek paper. The result is treated as synthesized processor evidence, not fabricated-silicon or complete DeepSeek deployment evidence.

Bident revision checks: the illustrative power products and harmonic mean were checked exactly; enumerating all five ordered four-leaf binary tree shapes and all leaf-weight permutations confirmed the example's minimum cost of 25. This is not a check of Bident's unrecovered model or proof. All 19 focused theme/link tests passed. The served article returned HTTP 200 with 76 resolving same-page links and no duplicate IDs, and the local-file link audit passed. The inaccessible original-paper mirror was replaced with the accessible Stanford-hosted Huffman paper and the page rebuilt. No independent reading review or full-suite rerun is claimed for this increment.

The PhotoMT continuation derives an eight-leaf dependency tree and a three-hash inclusion path, explains finite-field arithmetic and discrete-output recovery, and separates subtree interleaving from elimination of dependencies. It distinguishes tree throughput from complete-proof latency and identifies unresolved energy boundaries. Fresh paper-name and author searches did not recover a full manuscript or artifact. The tree, Poseidon, and microring background sources explain requirements, not the paper's undocumented implementation. A new explicit anchor connects the evidence card to the expanded discussion.

PhotoMT revision checks: the illustrative tree path was checked using a domain-separated SHA-256 construction, not Poseidon or PhotoMT, and the modular arithmetic was checked directly. After updating a prose-specific privacy-boundary assertion, all 19 focused theme/link tests passed. The served page returned HTTP 200 with 68 resolving same-page links and no duplicate IDs; the local-file audit passed for 469 Markdown files and 2,276 links. These checks establish navigation and example consistency, not hardware correctness, cryptographic security, or a new whole-suite checkpoint.

The active-calibration continuation explains estimates versus settings, growing uncertainty, dependencies that invalidate previous fits, experiment selection versus resource scheduling, and quality-conditioned maintenance intervals. It adds explicitly illustrative interval, information-rate, and task-scheduling calculations. Fresh title/author and artifact-name searches recovered the official DAC description but no matching manuscript or repository; detailed policy internals and numerical paper results remain unavailable. The paper card links back to the expanded explanation without changing its evidence tier or claiming artifact execution.

Calibration revision checks: 19 focused theme/link tests passed. The interval endpoints, information rates, dependency schedule, and subsequent two-state Bayesian example were checked arithmetically. After the final conceptual additions and rebuild, the served page returned HTTP 200 with 60 resolving same-page links and no duplicate IDs. The file-link audit passed for 469 Markdown files and 2,276 local links. This is neither an experimental reproduction nor a collection-wide comprehension review; the full suite was not rerun for this prose increment.

The NEAT continuation develops a three-qubit parity-check example, a hand-derived two-layer interaction schedule and lower bound, the effect of restoring physical movement constraints, and the difference between valid and proven-optimal schedules. It separates physical fault-free probability from logical memory failure and explains why an equal-gate-depth motion comparison is not an unrestricted comparison of complete compilation pipelines. These examples are explicitly educational derivations, not NEAT runs. The existing paper card now links back to this explanation.

NEAT revision checks: 19 focused theme/link tests passed; the hand-derived parity outcomes, gate-resource exclusions, and numerical probability were checked separately. After the final prose clarification and rebuild, the served article returned HTTP 200 with 52 resolving same-page links, two tables, and no duplicate IDs. The local-file audit checked 469 Markdown files and 2,276 links with no missing targets. These establish arithmetic and document structure, not independent validation of the quantum compiler or a passed collection-wide reading review.

The subsequent continuation expands NeuroPDE with a five-position diffusion example, a first-step derivation of the random-walk estimator, the circuit's state-transition responsibilities, and separate explanations of sampling variation, probability bias, discretization error, and complete-solver accounting. The numerical example is explicitly not a reported experiment. The completion audit now marks explanatory depth as reopened rather than calling the editorial pass complete.

The observed-page builder no longer repeats every paper's problem and method immediately before its full evidence record. No unique evidence field is removed. Explicit explanation anchors connect the RISC-Q and NeuroPDE cards back to their authored discussions; other cards do not imply that this deeper rewrite has occurred. This navigation change addresses repetition and separation of prose from evidence, not the unfinished depth of the other papers.

Verification for the NeuroPDE continuation: 66 theme/link/scaffold tests and six direction/comparison tests passed. The arithmetic of the illustrative grid equations was checked separately. The served article returned HTTP 200 with 44 resolving same-page links and no duplicate IDs. The local-file link audit passed; these checks do not establish reader comprehension, independent paper acceptance, or a new whole-suite checkpoint. The previously interrupted current-evidence propagation is now built and covered by the direction/comparison tests; broader reconciliation remains open as described below.

The user's subsequent reading review rejects the existing depth and clarity. The earlier count of 113 authored subjects is a coverage checkpoint, not evidence that those subjects or their paper writeups are understandable. The explanatory-depth pass remains open across the collection.

Inspection of the neuromorphic/quantum article confirms a concrete failure: it named timed queues, memory-mapped input/output, and conditional-gate preloading without developing their execution sequence or resource tradeoffs. The generated paper cards still organize evidence fields; they are not substitutes for self-contained paper explanations. Source caveats are necessary but cannot replace an explanation of how the method works.

The current revision expands that article's foundations and RISC-Q discussion. It introduces inputs and outputs, physical representation, an explicitly illustrative end-to-end cost calculation, command and measurement flow, queue timing, preparation versus selection, and interpretation of clock and resource measurements. It is a worked editorial example, not a claim that all papers have been rewritten to this depth.

For the remaining pass, each paper needs a continuous explanation of the problem, required output, existing approach, actual change, step-by-step operation, reason for the expected benefit, added costs, and the experiment that tests the claim. Define necessary terms at first use. Distinguish explanatory examples from reported experiments. Locate factual paper claims at source pages and tables when accessible; an abstract-only record cannot support an invented detailed implementation. Each subtheme must explain the shared problem and meaningful branches before comparing papers. Historical evidence cards remain available for traceability, but must be labeled and treated as evidence records rather than finished teaching text.

Depth cannot be accepted by a word count, a heading checklist, a keyword test, or passing software tests. A reading review must determine whether the mechanism can be reconstructed from the prose and whether the claimed result follows under the stated conditions. No collection-wide comprehension review has passed. The verified current-reading overlay does not resolve the broader source-reconciliation requirements.

Verification of this example revision: the article builder completed, the served article returned HTTP 200, and the new foundation and RISC-Q section anchors appeared in its HTML. The focused theme/link suite passed 18 tests after updating an assertion for the rewritten engineering-time caveat. These are build and document checks only; the full suite and independent comprehension review were not performed for this revision.

This audit examined the ten theme articles, five cross-cutting subtheme articles, six direction articles, the expanded taxonomy, their generators, and the server that opens their Markdown links. It also checked whether readers could reach the complete evidence for each of the 113 observed subthemes.

## Findings and changes

### The introductions did not explain enough

The architecture article began with “workload structure” and “specialization” without explaining operations, stored values, dependencies, or why available arithmetic units can wait for data. The memory direction listed placement, locality, migration, topology, and contention in one sentence. A beginner had to understand the conclusion before reading its explanation.

The ten theme articles now develop the basic process, the limiting resource, the choices researchers change, and the comparison needed to establish a benefit. Six direction articles now explain the causal reasoning and what would support or weaken the hypothesis. The five cross-cutting pages include the relevant foundational explanation and links to their individual subjects.

### The pages treated technical identifiers as explanations

The theme and subtheme pages contained dense tables of hyphenated coding labels. The expanded taxonomy listed paper IDs without linking to an article about the observed subject. Only five cross-cutting subtheme pages existed, despite 113 unique observed coding labels.

Every observed subtheme now has a reading page with links to its parent subjects and all its captured paper evidence. The theme pages and browser explorer link directly to these pages. The machine labels remain available for searching and provenance.

### The generator removed necessary evidence

Paper cards clipped metrics, limitations, workload descriptions, and source locations to fixed character counts. The architecture article's MTIA 300 card, for example, ended measurement and limitation fields with an ellipsis. It also displayed source locations as a shortened representation of a data structure. Baselines were marked present in a table without being fully presented in the card.

The evidence renderer now prints the complete stored baseline, measurement, limitation, and locator fields as paragraphs. Lists and structured locations are rendered as readable entries. Missing page, section, and table references remain explicit. This restores captured information; it does not supply page references that have not been extracted.

### Trend wording exceeded the available time comparison

The direction articles used words such as “increasingly” while drawing on an uneven current sample and an ICCAD fallback year. The homepage also presented four directions even though six were defined.

The six rewritten articles describe hypotheses, the reasoning that connects the cases, and experiments that could weaken the claims. They explain the need for comparable editions before estimating change over time. The homepage now presents all six.

### Markdown was delivered as raw text

The server used the standard static-file handler for `.md` paths. Adding headings and collapsible HTML to the source could not make those links appear as formatted articles in the browser.

The server now renders Markdown as HTML at the existing URLs. Articles have a contents sidebar, a readable text width, responsive layout, table scrolling, print styles, and links back to themes, subthemes, and research directions. The Markdown files remain editable source documents.

## Remaining editorial and evidence limits

The detailed subtheme rewrite is active. Eleven subjects now have individually written explanations connecting technical reasoning to specific papers. These cover the initial six computation and design subjects plus memory hierarchy and persistence, storage and data movement, chiplet/on-chip interconnect, processing in memory, and memory/compute reliability. Their authored sections are maintained separately from generated evidence so rebuilding the site preserves the writing.

Three additional individual rewrites cover simulation/emulation/testing, formal analysis, and formal/functional verification, bringing the total to fourteen. These explain the relationship between requirements, test generation, modeled behavior, proof assumptions, and reported outcomes.

Three security and reliability subjects now also have individual explanations: side-channel/hardware security, hardware/system security, and accelerator reliability. The authored total is seventeen.

Placement/routing/floorplanning, placement/routing/timing, and process-aware design now also have individual explanations, bringing the authored total to twenty.

Language-model-assisted design flows, generative design/synthesis, and generative design automation now have individual explanations. The authored total is twenty-three.

Edge deployment, language-model systems/runtime, and distributed/disaggregated systems now have individual explanations, bringing the authored total to twenty-six.

Optimization/search methods, high-level synthesis/compilers, and logic synthesis/optimization now have individual explanations. The authored total is twenty-nine.

Three-dimensional stacking/thermal management, three-dimensional integration/thermal management, and thermal/reliability modeling now have individual explanations. The authored total is thirty-two. These distinguish thermal prediction, reconstruction, control, physical implementation, and reliability estimation rather than treating their metrics as comparable.

Learned EDA/optimization and graph learning for EDA now have individual explanations covering all fifteen records in those groups. The authored total is thirty-four. These separate prediction, search, proof suggestions, and training efficiency, with explicit validation requirements for each.

Manufacturing/yield, manufacturing/lithography, and manufacturing monitoring/yield now have individual explanations. The authored total is thirty-seven. These separate mask simulation, optimization, inspection, actual yield, and tool-chain attestation; the seven source records do not establish interchangeable outcomes.

Both privacy-preserving computation groups now have individual explanations covering six records. The authored total is thirty-nine. The articles explicitly distinguish encryption, encoding, multiparty protocols, and unspecified private-inference implementations rather than assigning them a common security guarantee.

Timing analysis/optimization and parasitic extraction/modeling now have individual explanations. The authored total is forty-one. The capacitance article incorporates the recovered manuscript's page-8 table/figure conditions, model-selection exposure, and preparation costs without importing conflicting project-page configurations.

Processor/RTL verification and verification/debugging tools now have individual explanations covering five records. The authored total is forty-three. These separate input generation, comparison validity, model fidelity, log interpretation, and independent patch correctness.

Analog layout/circuit automation and analog calibration/modeling now have individual explanations. The authored total is forty-five. These distinguish physical legality from electrical performance, relative from absolute accuracy, and one-week device measurements from ten-year drift simulation.

Simulation/emulation and simulation/numerical methods now have individual explanations covering four records. The authored total is forty-seven. These separate event semantics, trace validity, benchmark comparability, and numerical-solver correctness and cost.

Chiplet interconnects and advanced packaging/interconnect now have individual explanations covering four records. The authored total is forty-nine. These separate lossless coding, network design, routing legality, electrical acceptance, and fabrication evidence.

Compiler/runtime optimization and synthesis/compilation now have individual explanations covering four records. The authored total is fifty-one. These distinguish valid reuse, adaptation overhead, neutral-atom rearrangement, bounded exact synthesis, and full execution costs.

Both reliability/fault-tolerance groups now have individual explanations covering three records. The authored total is fifty-three. The SERA-Float article incorporates page-backed correction limits, fault-injection conditions, application degradation, and synthesis-accounting caveats.

Architecture modeling/exploration now has an individual explanation of its two records, distinguishing optimistic limits from implementable predictions and decision quality from average model error. The authored total is fifty-four.

Reproducibility/artifacts/benchmarks now has an individual CapBench explanation, distinguishing label agreement, model error, independent splits, and executable reproduction. The authored total is fifty-five.

Physical verification now has an individual DRCGen explanation, including boundary cases, independent expected results, false rejections, and cross-PDK transfer scope. The authored total is fifty-six.

Power integrity/reliability now has an individual GEMIR explanation, separating voltage and stress predictions, physical training constraints, relative prediction-error improvements, and actual reliability outcomes. The authored total is fifty-seven.

Package-system co-design now has an individual BLADE explanation, separating plane synthesis, metal-density constraints, electrical estimates, and physical validation. The authored total is fifty-eight.

Chiplet partitioning/integration now has an individual MAHL explanation, separating hierarchical generation, integration checks, best-case Pass@5, and selected-objective physical comparisons. The authored total is fifty-nine.

Hardware generation/compilation now has an individual FSGen explanation, distinguishing fusion, sparse metadata and scheduling, power-model accuracy, and complete generated-design validation. The authored total is sixty.

FPGA/reconfigurable design now has an individual explanation of dynamic-cost fracturable-LUT mapping, separating logical counts, physical resource use, runtime reconfiguration, and completed implementation quality. The authored total is sixty-one.

Emulation/prototyping now has an individual Boolean-processor emulation explanation, separating target semantics, platform execution rate, communication, capacity, and debugging visibility. The authored total is sixty-two.

Distributed/wafer-scale inference now has an individual BusyBarn explanation, preserving its artifact-description-only evidence boundary while explaining mapping, routing, collectives, faults, and ablations. The authored total is sixty-three.

Cross-layer design automation now has an individual L2L explanation, separating logical complexity, physical cell quality, library utility, and search/validation costs. The authored total is sixty-four.

Interconnect/networking now has an individual Credix explanation, separating credit accounting, distributed capacity, overload, latency, fairness, and hardware feasibility. The authored total is sixty-five.

Power/thermal design now has an individual Synchoros explanation, explicitly limiting the record to CGRA energy estimation and separating mapping, measurement boundary, calibration, and decision quality. The authored total is sixty-six.

Signal processing/sensing now has an individual AI+SAR graph-optimization explanation, distinguishing dependencies, transfer costs, numerical agreement, and application quality without inventing unreported transformations. The authored total is sixty-seven.

Scientific/engineering computing now has an individual GPU LU-factorization explanation, distinguishing dependency scheduling, fill-in, preparation costs, numerical validity, and complete simulation outcomes. The authored total is sixty-eight.

Analog circuit optimization now has an individual COmPOSER explanation, separating feasibility, competing electrical objectives, synthesis scope, physical validation, and search budget. The authored total is sixty-nine.

Compilation/mapping now has an individual RAM-CGRA explanation, distinguishing placement, scheduling, time-dependent reachability, mapping success, and execution efficiency. The authored total is seventy-two.

RF/mixed-signal design now has an individual ASG-MOENN explanation, distinguishing predistortion, expert selection, operating-condition adaptation, measured signal quality, and real-time cost. The authored total is seventy-three.

Physical design/routing now has an individual explanation of track/layer reassignment and PCB power/signal allocation, separating geometric completion from electrical and manufacturing acceptance. The authored total is seventy-five. Both source records still lack recovered paper pages and numerical validation.

A newly recovered iHyperG manuscript supplies Sections 4–6 and Table 1 conditions, including randomly enlarged benchmarks and a full-rebuild baseline. Refreshed official descriptions add ATOM-3D and module-estimation results without promoting them to manuscript-level evidence. Older captured cards remain explicitly identified as historical where superseded by the editorial recovery.

These distinguish model-based exploration from complete synthesis, three-class from full CIFAR-10 accuracy, numerical from measurement error, and the DAC ADC paper from its ICCAD predecessor. These are not interchangeable timing guarantees.

It distinguishes analog matching from digital inference, adaptive matching from guaranteed correction, peak INT8 from mixed-precision results, and DAC 2026 from the ICCAD 2025 fallback. Separate power and throughput outcomes, imperfect throughput predictions, kernel-timing boundaries, and unchecked final-version equivalence are explicit; temperature reduction is not presented as energy or lifetime savings.

Device modeling/simulation now has an individual explanation of WF-PINN and IGNITE, bringing the authored total to eighty-four. Emerging-device design now has an individual explanation of superconducting retiming/interface redesign and JSPlace, bringing the authored total to eighty-five. It separates logic families, junction counts from area/energy, cycle time from end-to-end latency, and placement length objectives from final pulse-timing validation. Biochips and specialized systems now has an individual explanation, bringing the authored total to eighty-six. LiPCon's recovered manuscript supports page-backed fabrication conditions and separates original-platform accuracy from cross-printer transfer. The audit identifies a twelve-case dataset-count discrepancy and unresolved chip/augmentation split independence. Pressure-path scheduling remains description-backed, without a recovered manuscript or numerical comparison.

AI accelerator microarchitecture now has an individual explanation of AMBER, MBA, AHASD, and OASIS, bringing the authored total to eighty-seven. OASIS version 4 supplies PDF pages 9–11 for simulation/synthesis conditions, precision-mismatched FIGLUT comparisons, and accuracy tables. Its arXiv abstract (1.98%), downloaded PDF abstract (1.94%), and body (2.05%) disagree on the accuracy headline; the article preserves this discrepancy instead of selecting a convenient average. The earlier KLLM version is not used to supply OASIS performance claims. Three DAC manuscripts remain missing.

Chiplet system architecture now has an individual explanation of DUET, ChiPlanner, and ChiSER, bringing the authored total to eighty-eight. DUET's recovered version-1 manuscript supplies pages 4–6 for simulated baselines, unequal memory resources, matched-throughput assumptions without explicit multi-request scheduling, and scaled area estimates. Its table/prose discrepancy is retained. Refreshed official descriptions supply ChiPlanner timing and ChiSER composite-objective results, which are not rewritten as timing closure or separate energy savings. Their full manuscripts remain missing.

Wafer-scale and heterogeneous integration now has an individual explanation of HyperWafer and WSC-Cost, bringing the authored total to eighty-nine. Refreshed descriptions provide hypergraph/routing mechanisms and four separate speed comparisons, plus stitching/assembly cost scope and a stated prototype-validation claim. The article separates sparse arithmetic from communication, setup from execution, and manufacturing cost from usable-system performance. Full manuscripts and quantitative validation conditions remain missing; related publications are not substituted for them.

Chiplets and heterogeneous integration now has an individual HBSpec explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety. It connects branch computation and state lifetime to logic-die processing, bank mapping, and scheduling. Refreshed official performance factors are kept separate and are not called energy gains or defined latency gains; measurement definitions, branch-management details, and the full manuscript remain missing. Related H2-LLM and adaptive-drafting papers are not used to supply HBSpec results.

Formal and equivalence verification now has an individual four-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety-one. The recovered LUT-mapping manuscript supplies page 5 conditions, distinguishes PAR2 from solved counts, and includes mapping overhead. Refreshed IC3 and PRS descriptions supply separate solve/runtime results. The article corrects the interpretation of LUT mapping as necessarily hardware implementation and of approximate reachability as necessarily missing bugs. Three manuscripts and independent proof/reproduction checks remain missing.

The chapter download returned 403, so page-backed experimental conditions remain unavailable. Reliable and efficient compute now has an individual ADA/ADA-Plus explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety-three. The recovered manuscript provides pages 3–6 for width control, synthesis/simulation conditions, small-sample power evaluation, and separate nominal-voltage/minimum-effective-voltage comparisons. Application accuracy is not called zero timing errors. Higher-mode sign assumptions and inconsistent mode numbering remain explicitly unresolved.

Reliable and secure compute now has an individual CRYSTALS arithmetic-protection explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety-four. The article separates detection from correction and safe handling, explains residue ambiguity, and keeps checker faults and attacker-selectable patterns within the required evaluation questions. Full manuscript, correction guarantees, and exact fault conditions remain missing.

Confidential and trusted computing now has an individual migration explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety-five. It separates execution compatibility from security-policy compatibility, attestation from authorization, and downtime from total migration time. The refreshed description supports an AMD SEV/Hygon CSV prototype, not universal x86 TEE migration or numerical performance guarantees. The old card's suggestion of undocumented firmware dependence is explicitly unverified. Full manuscript, page-backed protocol conditions, and failure analysis remain missing.

Reliable and secure AI systems now has an individual WFH-BFs explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety-six. It separates random faults from selected attacks, pruning from exact repair, and arithmetic counts from measured latency or energy. Refreshed official evidence supplies the three-tier mechanism and attack-cost/accuracy claims, with undefined cost units and error conditions explicitly retained. Full manuscript, page-backed evaluation, and independent fault-injection checks remain missing.

Neuromorphic and emerging architectures now has an individual UniSpike explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety-seven. The recovered version-1 manuscript supplies pages 2–4 for scheduling barriers, packet assembly, simulation configuration, area modeling, and distinct traffic/speed/energy comparisons. The article distinguishes address sharing from event deletion, logical time from transfer time, and conventional-baseline ratios from comparisons against every competitor. Independent reproduction and fabricated-hardware validation remain unestablished.

Hardware security validation now has an individual three-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety-eight. AttackonCTF's recovered version-1 manuscript supplies pages 4–6 for benchmark conditions, checking methods, and separate recall/precision results. Its “accuracy” means precision; the conclusion's detection-reduction headline and inference about internal model reasoning are not silently accepted. Refreshed instruction-discovery and DefVul-Risk descriptions retain missing processor, classifier, CVE, and tool-version conditions. Independent reproduction and the other two manuscripts remain missing.

Generated coverage cautions now distinguish single-paper case studies from multi-paper subjects, and the record-count sentence uses singular grammar where appropriate. A regression test checks this wording against the actual paper anchors across all 113 observed pages. This fixes presentation consistency without promoting sample size into longitudinal evidence.

Emerging and non-von Neumann compute now has an individual PMTPA, EHPC, and iADS explanation, bringing the authored total to ninety-nine. The article separates objective energy from electrical energy, solution area from accelerator area, and noise-generation cost from full diffusion execution. EHPC's 2025 poster is not used to supply the different 2026 speed range. All three manuscripts and page-backed conditions remain missing.

Analog compute-in-memory now has an individual four-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred. It flags the digital LUT-CiM taxonomy mismatch, separates vehicle recovery from computation latency, and distinguishes sigmoid variation tolerance from general robustness. The author-uploaded LUT manuscript supplies Sections 4.1–4.2 and Tables 1–3 for quantized versus full-precision quality and modeled hardware/comparator conditions; exact PDF-page mapping remains unverified after the download failed. Other manuscripts and independent device-to-task validation remain missing.

Embedded and edge systems now has an individual four-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and one. The article separates exact symbol decoding from lossless images and checkpoint correctness from classification quality. Manuscripts, page-backed experiments, source-record reconciliation, and independent reproduction remain open.

Secure and robust ML now has an individual four-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and two. Recovered STRAP-ViT and Flip-Agent version-1 manuscripts supply page-backed evaluation limits: an impossible extracted Top-5 entry, clean-performance reductions, adaptive-attack scope, precise fault-access assumptions, and output agreement versus task correctness. CipherShield leakage measurements and Runtime-ADAR's homomorphic-encryption scope remain description-backed. Missing manuscripts, source discrepancies, and independent reproduction remain open.

Photonic and optical compute now has an individual four-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and three. Three recovered manuscripts provide page-backed distinctions between architecture simulation, noise injection, and physical-response design testing. ROSA's extracted Table 4 percentages need arithmetic reconciliation; Light-Bound Transformers' efficiency, energy, and adjusted latency comparisons require aligned accounting; SP2RINT's unavailable completed baseline limits its headline speedup. LightningFNO's refreshed description reports fabricated prototypes missing from the old card. Source-record reconciliation, camera-ready equivalence, and independent reproduction remain open.

Reliable and secure ML now has an individual four-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and four. It distinguishes accidental faults from attacker resistance, activation outliers from errors, selective repetition from sparsity benefits, and embedded code capacity from free protection. The recovered DRIFT manuscript supplies pages 3–6 for error-free memory assumptions, approximate substitution, synthesis/simulation, and separate energy/speed operating points. The other manuscripts, E³-CODE's lifetime model, source-card reconciliation, and independent reproduction remain open.

Quantum hardware and control now has an individual four-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and five. Three version-1 manuscripts provide page-backed limits on FPGA readout, simulated break-even, and logical routing. The article separates algorithmic fidelity break-even from classical advantage, color-code teleportation from physical motion, and residual routing overhead from total depth. LUNA's reused evaluation split and inconsistent shift range remain explicit. NIAQ's one-day-equivalent dataset and post-synthesis claims are not treated as continuous fabricated-device operation. Camera-ready equivalence, source-record reconciliation, and independent reproduction remain open.

Datacenter and cloud systems now has an individual five-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and six. Recovered eLLM, HW-Router, and GreenLLM manuscripts provide page-backed distinctions between decode and total throughput, added and total routing cost, homogeneous tested GPUs and heterogeneous motivation, and energy savings versus latency pass-rate losses. HW-Router's queue-skew interpretation and GreenLLM's blanket violation claim need reconciliation. GREEN's repository and simulated scale are separated from physical deployment and reproduction; sMoE's billing and startup conditions remain missing. Source-card reconciliation and independent evaluation remain open.

The eLLM, HW-Router, and GreenLLM reading cards now incorporate the recovered manuscript conditions and exact page locations through a dated [correction register](../data/editorial-paper-corrections-v1.json). Their original captured fields remain available in expandable sections. These three corrections update the browser and Markdown reading pages without changing historical adjudication inputs, taxonomy, or scientific acceptance. Other paper-card corrections remain open; the presence of an authored introduction does not establish that all underlying cards have been reconciled.

Memory and data movement now has an individual six-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and seven. It derives capacity, bandwidth, latency, representation, sharing, contention, and recomputation from value lifetimes. Six reading-card corrections incorporate refreshed official descriptions and the Cachence author abstract; the correction register now covers nine papers. No full manuscript was recovered in this batch. REACT's thesis discovery returned an invalid certificate or a JavaScript landing page, and Cachence's DOI route did not yield readable paper text. Description sections are cited explicitly instead of invented page numbers. Full experimental conditions and independent evaluation remain open.

Edge, embedded, and mobile systems now has an individual six-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and eight. Three recovered manuscripts distinguish hard deadlines from PHAROS's bounded-response soft-real-time condition, measured kernel emulation from single-DAG analysis, and MATCHA block-level speedups from complete models without improvement. Three corresponding card corrections bring the register to twelve papers. PHAROS's EDF guarantee is qualified by preemption overhead; the GPU paper has a slower Stencil case. Pegasus and RTFL manuscripts remain unrecovered; SatSFL's discovered PDF failed DNS resolution and its DOI route did not produce readable full text. Independent review and final-version matching remain open.

Robotics and edge AI now has an individual seven-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and nine. Three recovered manuscripts distinguish model latency from control timing, trainable parameters from training time, and Atari training from robot deployment. ActionFlow's time-unit and platform-comparison issues, DySL-VLA's inconsistent baseline length, and SwitchMT's lower scores on two games remain explicit. Refreshed descriptions correct ENACT's field interpretation and add bounded SpRDA, HIP-MaN, and PS-CAM mechanisms. All seven cards are corrected, bringing the register to nineteen papers. Four manuscripts, final-version matching, and independent evaluation remain open.

Quantum architectures and control now has an individual nine-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and ten. Five recovered manuscripts separate gate count, schedule depth, modeled fidelity, image-analysis timing, and compression. The transport compiler's fidelity–duration tradeoff, equal-depth syndrome schedules' different error rates, and CryoZip's inconsistent peak-rate condition remain explicit. All nine cards are corrected, bringing the register to twenty-eight papers. Four manuscripts remain unrecovered after title/author searches; author tool and repository listings do not count as executed artifacts. Original coding outside the reading-card overlay, final-version matching, and independent reproduction remain open.

Specialized AI accelerators now has an individual nine-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and eleven. Four recovered manuscripts establish GPU timing, FPGA graph processing, and modeled MASQ/DSPE hardware conditions. MASQ's mixed quality changes, GEMM-GS's matrix-dimension inconsistency, and DSPE's conflicting efficiency headline and comparator citations remain explicit. Five card corrections bring the overlay register to thirty-three papers. Five manuscripts, original-code reconciliation, final-version matching, and independent reproduction remain open.

Multimedia and edge systems now has an individual nine-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and twelve. Five matching manuscripts distinguish reconstruction, rendering, ranking, video answering, and generation. AdaGScale's test-view tuning, MIRAGE's excluded query decomposition, LiveVLM's first-token timing and accuracy tradeoff, ORBIS's slower matching ablation, and GalaxyDiT's inconsistent speed headline remain explicit. ExCave's author repository was inspected but not executed; an anonymous same-title submission does not establish final manuscript identity. Nine card corrections bring the overlay register to forty-two papers. Four matching manuscripts, original-code reconciliation, independent review, and historical comparability remain open.

Neuromorphic, quantum, and non-von Neumann systems now has an individual eight-paper explanation, bringing the authored total to one hundred and thirteen. Three inspected manuscripts distinguish RISC-Q's channel/resource comparisons from feedback latency, NEAT's motion and depth exceptions from blanket optimality, and NeuroPDE's simulated single-walk cost and squared reference error from complete-solver timing and statistical variance. Three card corrections bring the overlay register to forty-five papers. Five full texts remain unrecovered, and NEAT's author-hosted draft still has anonymous publisher metadata. Original-code reconciliation, independent review, and historical comparability remain open.

All 113 observed subjects now have individual authored explanations. This completes the initial subject-by-subject editorial pass, not the end-to-end research goal. It does not establish that all papers have full-text evidence, that the taxonomy is final, or that the underlying coding and synthesis have been reconciled with every correction.

Paper cards now also expose applicable supplementary source assessments from the existing assessment index. These preserve their recorded dates, source links, extraction scope, experimental conditions, and uncertainty separately from the original coding. An original record's missing page reference must therefore be read alongside any later assessment, rather than treated as the final state of source recovery.

The conceptual theme and direction sections were rewritten as explanations. The 113 observed-subtheme pages now contain individual authored explanations as well as captured paper evidence; they are not 113 independently reviewed literature reviews. Uncorrected paper summaries retain specialist terms from the original coding. A reading guide defines common terms, but comprehensive simplification and evidence reconciliation of every paper still require checking each mechanism against its primary text.

The coding contains labels that may be aliases and labels with a single example. This update preserves those boundaries for review. It does not establish that every label denotes an independent research field. Historical recovery reports, machine registers, and evidence dossiers remain research records; they were not all rewritten as introductory essays.

No new paper result or scientific acceptance is claimed by the rendering or editorial checks. Unspecified experimental conditions, missing page locations, and independent review remain substantive research work.

## Next evidence-reconciliation work

The next pass must improve explanatory depth and reconcile evidence rather than count more pages. The current-reading overlay now feeds observed pages, parent-page selections, direction evidence dossiers, and comparison displays. Registered corrections in those dossiers use current locators, preserve the historical record, and do not inherit a historical complete-condition label as approval. The original ledgers read by `read_selected_sample`, downstream coverage, review packets, and broader prose synthesis are not thereby reconciled. Identify each remaining dependent claim and do not reinterpret a correction as independent acceptance.

Prioritize the 45 registered corrections and known article/card disagreements, then audit the ten parent themes and cross-cutting directions for unsupported generalization. Check aliases and misclassified mechanisms against primary sources. Expand technically coded coverage beyond the DAC-heavy sample, pursue equivalent historical evidence, and keep unavailable-source attempts and independent-review gaps visible. Completing the 113 articles does not remove any of those requirements.
